========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, um, I came in because I just... I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark hearing the rain, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way. Which, honestly, the rain has been crazy lately. My buddy's basement actually flooded last week and completely ruined his Xbox setup. We used to play down there all the time back in the day before I left school to start working. 

But anyway, when I finally do pass out, I just wake up all sweaty from these dreams. Then I have to drag myself to the call center, and I show up feeling like I never even slept. And let me tell you, doing customer service when you're that exhausted is just... it's brutal. Like yesterday, I had this lady screaming at me for twenty minutes over a blender return, like I personally built the thing and broke it on purpose just to mess up her smoothies. People get so crazy over kitchen appliances. But yeah, the sleep thing is mostly why I made the appointment.

I've been super into this one survival game lately where you have to build your own base and scavenge for supplies. It's crazy how detailed they make games now, man. Like, the grass actually bends when your character walks through it. \n\nMy computer's graphics card was struggling so bad with it last month, I actually had to buy a used one off Craigslist. I met the guy in a Wendy's parking lot, which was honestly kind of sketchy, but he gave me a really good deal. I ended up just going inside and getting a Baconator while I was there. Have you had their fries since they changed the recipe a while back? They're way crispier now, they keep the skin on them or whatever. I used to hate their fries, but now they're probably top tier. But, um... wait, sorry. What did you ask me? Something about my hobbies?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble finding pleasure in things you usually enjoy, even when you're not tired?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel down really. I mean, I'm just tired, you know? Like, I still enjoy the stuff I like. I actually just watched this whole documentary series on Netflix about deep-sea diving, which was wild. Like, the pressure down there is so intense it can just crush a submarine like a soda can. It makes you think about how much of the ocean we haven't even explored yet. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which is crazy when you think about it, because the moon is literally in space. \n\nI actually wanted to be an astronaut when I was a little kid. I had this glow-in-the-dark star map on my ceiling that my mom helped me put up. They kept falling off though, because she used this cheap tape from the dollar store. We used to go to that dollar store all the time, the one over by the old mall? They had these off-brand candies that tasted like straight chemicals but I loved them anyway. I think the mall is mostly empty now, just like, a weird shoe store and a pretzel place. I haven't been there in years. But yeah, no, I don't feel depressed or anything, just exhausted from the sleep stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel excessively sleepy during the day, or do you feel unrefreshed even after you wake up?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I feel sleepy all day. Like I said, I drag myself to the call center and I'm just zoning out at my desk. Which is super dangerous because my supervisor, Gary, he literally paces the aisles with a clipboard. He thinks he's some kind of drill sergeant, but he's just a guy who wears too much cologne. It's that really strong, spicy kind that gives you a headache if you stand too close. I don't know why guys think they need to bathe in the stuff. \n\nMy uncle used to wear this awful aftershave that smelled like pine needles and gasoline. We used to go fishing with him up at this lake a couple hours north. The mosquitoes up there were the size of small birds, I swear. I got bit so bad one time my eye swelled completely shut. We had to go to this tiny urgent care clinic in the middle of nowhere, and the doctor looked like he was about a hundred years old. He gave me this weird pink cream that smelled like bubblegum. It actually worked, though. I wonder if they still make that stuff. \n\nBut, uh, yeah, unrefreshed is a good word for it. I wake up and it's like I didn't even sleep. Just totally drained.\n\nDoctor: Do you snore loudly or has anyone ever noticed you stop breathing briefly during your sleep?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I live by myself right now anyway, so there's no one to really tell me. Well, it's just me and my cat, Barnaby. He actually snores sometimes, which is hilarious because he's so small. I found him behind the dumpster at my old apartment complex. That place was an absolute nightmare, honestly. The walls were like paper and my upstairs neighbor used to vacuum at like 3 AM. Who vacuums at 3 AM? I think he worked night shifts at the bottling plant across town or something. \n\nMy cousin worked at a bottling plant for a while, making those weird flavored sparkling waters. He told me they literally just use a tiny dropper of flavor for like a hundred gallons of water. It's such a rip-off when you think about it, paying three bucks for a can of static electricity. I usually just drink tap water anyway, I got one of those filter pitchers. But, um... wait, what did you ask? Snoring? Yeah, no, I don't think I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel as though your sleep isn't restful, even when you do manage to sleep for a decent amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. That's actually why I came in, because I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. I'll just lie there for hours, hearing the rain against the window, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way... and then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from these intense dreams. \n\nI used to love the rain, actually. When I was a kid, my grandma had this tin roof on her back porch and we'd sit out there just listening to it. She used to make this incredible sweet tea, like, diabetes in a glass, but it was so good. She kept it in this giant glass pitcher with yellow lemons painted on the side. I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. I've been meaning to use my phone instead, but I always forget to charge it. \n\nI actually need to get a new charger cable because Barnaby chewed through my last one. Yeah, foggy is a good word for it. Like I said, I came in because I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, hearing the rain or just... thinking about the bluff giving way, you know? And then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from those dreams. The guy in the cubicle next to me, Gary, he leans back so far I swear he's gonna snap the thing in half one day. Gary's always bringing in these weird smelling lunches, too. Like microwaved fish or something. I don't know how anyone can eat that at 11 in the morning. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I mean... I feel like we're just saying the same thing over and over, doc. But yes. Like I said, I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there for hours, listening to the rain or my brain just goes right back to the bluff giving way. And when I finally do fall asleep, I just wake up drenched in sweat from these awful dreams. So by the time I have to drag myself into the call center, it feels like I didn't sleep a single minute. 

Speaking of the call center, they just changed our headsets again. The new ones have this foam earpiece that makes my ear itch like crazy. I tried swapping it out with the old one, but the IT guy got super mad about it. He's this really intense dude who wears suspenders every day. Who wears suspenders to fix computers? He also collects those little bobblehead figures. His whole desk is covered in them. Every time you walk by, they all start nodding at you. It's honestly a little unnerving. 

But yeah, to answer your question, waking up feeling rested is just... not a thing for me anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it happens when I'm trying to sleep, but honestly, it happens during the day, too. Stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll hear the sound of rocks sliding, or I'll feel my leg stuck under me again, and for a minute I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting at my desk with a headset on. 

It's really embarrassing when it happens at work because I'll just freeze up while I'm on a call. The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. People always threaten to call the Better Business Bureau like it's the FBI or something. Half the time they don't even know what it is. Anyway, she was yelling, and suddenly I just... I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. 

My supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. 

But yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. 

I used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you.

The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. People always threaten to call the Better Business Bureau like it's the FBI or something. Half the time they don't even know what it is. Anyway, she was yelling, and suddenly I just... I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. \n\nMy supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. \n\nI used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know what else to say about the bluff. It happened, and now I'm just trying to deal with it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do these memories or images of the bluff feel very real or as if you're reliving the experience?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they feel completely real. Like I said before, it's like stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. I'll hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I'll literally feel my leg pinned under me, and for a second I'm right back on that trail. It's not like I'm just remembering it, I'm actually there, instead of sitting at my desk with my stupid headset on. \n\nWhich, by the way, I still haven't gotten the old earpiece back. The IT guy—Gary is his name, I finally asked—he actually put a lock on the supply cabinet. A padlock. Like we're going to steal foam earpieces and sell them on the black market or something. He was eating a tuna sandwich when I went to talk to him about it. The whole IT office smelled like fish. I don't understand people who bring fish to an office. It's just common courtesy, you know? Like microwaving broccoli. Just don't do it. My old roommate used to microwave broccoli at like 2 AM. He worked third shift at a warehouse. \n\nBut yeah, when the memories happen, it's... it's like I'm right back there on the dirt, feeling the whole thing happen all over again.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you have nightmares about what happened on the bluff, or do you often dream about it when you sleep?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 21 / customer_service_representative / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Sleep_Disturbance\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I barely sleep right anymore—I'll lie there hearing rain or thinking about the bluff giving way, then when I do pass out I wake up sweaty from dreams and show up to the call center feeling like I never slept.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually, that's kind of the main reason I came in. I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, thinking about the bluff giving way, or just... hearing the rain. 

Which, by the way, the roof at my apartment building is an absolute joke. Every time it rains, it sounds like the water is coming right through the ceiling. I've told my landlord, Rick, about it like three times. Rick is completely useless. He drives this massive lifted truck that takes up two parking spots, but he can't be bothered to fix a simple leak. He just told me to put a bucket in the hallway. A bucket! Like I'm paying rent to live in a cave. I swear, landlords get away with murder. My neighbor down the hall has a dog that barks at the wall all day, and Rick won't do anything about that either. 

But anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to pass out, I have these dreams about the trail. I'll wake up completely sweaty, just drenched, and then a few hours later I have to drag myself out of bed and show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. And then I have to sit there dealing with Brenda and Gary and all the screaming customers on zero sleep. It's exhausting.

He told me they literally just use a tiny dropper of flavor for like a hundred gallons of water. It's such a rip-off when you think about it, paying three bucks for a can of static electricity. I usually just drink tap water anyway, I got one of those filter pitchers. But, um... wait, what did you ask? Snoring? Yeah, no, I don't think I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel as though your sleep isn't restful, even when you do manage to sleep for a decent amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. That's actually why I came in, because I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. I'll just lie there for hours, hearing the rain against the window, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way... and then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from these intense dreams. \n\nI used to love the rain, actually. When I was a kid, my grandma had this tin roof on her back porch and we'd sit out there just listening to it. She used to make this incredible sweet tea, like, diabetes in a glass, but it was so good. She kept it in this giant glass pitcher with yellow lemons painted on the side. I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do manage to sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's pretty much what I just said. Like, I'll get maybe three, four hours if I'm lucky, and I still feel like I just ran a marathon when my alarm goes off. My alarm clock is actually this old radio I got at a garage sale. It's got this really harsh buzzing sound that just jars you awake. I've been meaning to use my phone instead, but I always forget to charge it. \n\nI actually need to get a new charger cable because Barnaby chewed through my last one. He's got this weird obsession with cords. I had to wrap all the lamp cords in that plastic tubing stuff, which was a huge pain to put on. I bought it at the hardware store down the street, the one next to that bakery that makes those amazing cheese danishes. Man, I haven't had one of those in ages. The lady who runs it is always so nice, she always gives me an extra one if I come in right before they close. \n\nBut yeah, the sleep thing. It's just... it's never enough. I always feel completely wiped out, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that even after sleeping, your mind feels foggy or your body feels heavy and tired throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, foggy is a good word for it. Like I said, I came in because I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, hearing the rain or just... thinking about the bluff giving way, you know? And then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from those dreams. So yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never slept at all. The new ones have this foam earpiece that makes my ear itch like crazy. I tried swapping it out with the old one, but the IT guy got super mad about it. He's this really intense dude who wears suspenders every day. Who wears suspenders to fix computers? He also collects those little bobblehead figures. His whole desk is covered in them. Every time you walk by, they all start nodding at you. It's honestly a little unnerving. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, waking up feeling rested is just... not a thing for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have upsetting or disturbing thoughts or images running through your mind while trying to fall asleep, like flashbacks or vivid memories that make it hard to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens when I'm trying to sleep, but honestly, it happens during the day, too. Stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll hear the sound of rocks sliding, or I'll feel my leg stuck under me again, and for a minute I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting at my desk with a headset on. \n\nIt's really embarrassing when it happens at work because I'll just freeze up while I'm on a call. The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. People always threaten to call the Better Business Bureau like it's the FBI or something. Half the time they don't even know what it is. Anyway, she was yelling, and suddenly I just... I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. \n\nMy supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. \n\nI used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Yeah, I mean... I definitely won't go near the coast trails now. Not happening. And my hiking buddies keep texting me to hang out or hit a different spot, but I just dodge their texts. Like, dude, I sit at a desk getting yelled at about billing errors all day, I don't need a tactical phone case. I just wanted a normal screen protector. I've been super into this one survival game lately where you have to build your own base and scavenge for supplies. It's crazy how detailed they make games now, man. Like, the grass actually bends when your character walks through it. \n\nMy computer's graphics card was struggling so bad with it last month, I actually had to buy a used one off Craigslist. I met the guy in a Wendy's parking lot, which was honestly kind of sketchy, but he gave me a really good deal. I ended up just going inside and getting a Baconator while I was there. Have you had their fries since they changed the recipe a while back? They're way crispier now, they keep the skin on them or whatever. I used to hate their fries, but now they're probably top tier. But, um... wait, sorry. What did you ask me? Something about my hobbies?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble finding pleasure in things you usually enjoy, even when you're not tired?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel down really. I mean, I'm just tired, you know? Like, I still enjoy the stuff I like. I actually just watched this whole documentary series on Netflix about deep-sea diving, which was wild. Like, the pressure down there is so intense it can just crush a submarine like a soda can. It makes you think about how much of the ocean we haven't even explored yet. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which is crazy when you think about it, because the moon is literally in space. \n\nI actually wanted to be an astronaut when I was a little kid. I had this glow-in-the-dark star map on my ceiling that my mom helped me put up. They kept falling off though, because she used this cheap tape from the dollar store. We used to go to that dollar store all the time, the one over by the old mall? They had these off-brand candies that tasted like straight chemicals but I loved them anyway. I think the mall is mostly empty now, just like, a weird shoe store and a pretzel place. I haven't been there in years. But yeah, no, I don't feel depressed or anything, just exhausted from the sleep stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel excessively sleepy during the day, or do you feel unrefreshed even after you wake up?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I feel sleepy all day. Like I said, I drag myself to the call center and I'm just zoning out at my desk. Which is super dangerous because my supervisor, Gary, he literally paces the aisles with a clipboard. He thinks he's some kind of drill sergeant, but he's just a guy who wears too much cologne. It's that really strong, spicy kind that gives you a headache if you stand too close. I don't know why guys think they need to bathe in the stuff. \n\nMy uncle used to wear this awful aftershave that smelled like pine needles and gasoline. We used to go fishing with him up at this lake a couple hours north. The mosquitoes up there were the size of small birds, I swear. I got bit so bad one time my eye swelled completely shut. We had to go to this tiny urgent care clinic in the middle of nowhere, and the doctor looked like he was about a hundred years old. He gave me this weird pink cream that smelled like bubblegum. It actually worked, though. I wonder if they still make that stuff. \n\nBut, uh, yeah, unrefreshed is a good word for it. I wake up and it's like I didn't even sleep. Just totally drained.\n\nDoctor: Do you snore loudly or has anyone ever noticed you stop breathing briefly during your sleep?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I live by myself right now anyway, so there's no one to really tell me. Well, it's just me and my cat, Barnaby. He actually snores sometimes, which is hilarious because he's so small. I found him behind the dumpster at my old apartment complex. That place was an absolute nightmare, honestly. The walls were like paper and my upstairs neighbor used to vacuum at like 3 AM. Who vacuums at 3 AM? I think he worked night shifts at the bottling plant across town or something. \n\nMy cousin worked at a bottling plant for a while, making those weird flavored sparkling waters. He told me they literally just use a tiny dropper of flavor for like a hundred gallons of water. It's such a rip-off when you think about it, paying three bucks for a can of static electricity. I usually just drink tap water anyway, I got one of those filter pitchers. But, um... wait, what did you ask? Snoring? Yeah, no, I don't think I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel as though your sleep isn't restful, even when you do manage to sleep for a decent amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. That's actually why I came in, because I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. I'll just lie there for hours, hearing the rain against the window, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way... and then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from these intense dreams. \n\nI used to love the rain, actually. When I was a kid, my grandma had this tin roof on her back porch and we'd sit out there just listening to it. She used to make this incredible sweet tea, like, diabetes in a glass, but it was so good. She kept it in this giant glass pitcher with yellow lemons painted on the side. I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do manage to sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's pretty much what I just said. Like, I'll get maybe three, four hours if I'm lucky, and I still feel like I just ran a marathon when my alarm goes off. My alarm clock is actually this old radio I got at a garage sale. It's got this really harsh buzzing sound that just jars you awake. I've been meaning to use my phone instead, but I always forget to charge it. \n\nI actually need to get a new charger cable because Barnaby chewed through my last one. He's got this weird obsession with cords. I had to wrap all the lamp cords in that plastic tubing stuff, which was a huge pain to put on. I bought it at the hardware store down the street, the one next to that bakery that makes those amazing cheese danishes. Man, I haven't had one of those in ages. The lady who runs it is always so nice, she always gives me an extra one if I come in right before they close. \n\nBut yeah, the sleep thing. It's just... it's never enough. I always feel completely wiped out, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I feel like we're going in circles here, doc, but yes. That's literally why I made the appointment. I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. I'll just lie there for hours, hearing the rain against the window, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way... and then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from these intense dreams. \n\nI used to love the rain, actually. When I was a kid, my grandma had this tin roof on her back porch and we'd sit out there just listening to it. She used to make this incredible sweet tea, like, diabetes in a glass, but it was so good. She kept it in this giant glass pitcher with yellow lemons painted on the side. I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that even after sleeping, your mind feels foggy or your body feels heavy and tired throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, foggy is a good word for it. Like I said, I came in because I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, hearing the rain or just... thinking about the bluff giving way, you know? And then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from those dreams. So yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never slept at all. My body just feels like lead. \n\nHonestly, sitting in that chair all day doesn't help. The chairs at work are terrible. They're those cheap mesh ones that squeak every time you shift your weight. The guy in the cubicle next to me, Gary, he leans back so far I swear he's gonna snap the thing in half one day. Gary's always bringing in these weird smelling lunches, too. Like microwaved fish or something. I don't know how anyone can eat that at 11 in the morning. I usually just grab a bag of chips from the vending machine, but half the time the coils get stuck and you have to shake the whole machine. \n\nBut yeah, my mind is definitely foggy. It's like trying to think through mud all day.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do manage to sleep for a few hours, do you wake up feeling as though you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I feel like we're just saying the same thing over and over, doc. But yes. Like I said, I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there for hours, listening to the rain or my brain just goes right back to the bluff giving way. And when I finally do fall asleep, I just wake up drenched in sweat from these awful dreams. So by the time I have to drag myself into the call center, it feels like I didn't sleep a single minute. \n\nSpeaking of the call center, they just changed our headsets again. The new ones have this foam earpiece that makes my ear itch like crazy. I tried swapping it out with the old one, but the IT guy got super mad about it. He's this really intense dude who wears suspenders every day. Who wears suspenders to fix computers? He also collects those little bobblehead figures. His whole desk is covered in them. Every time you walk by, they all start nodding at you. It's honestly a little unnerving. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, waking up feeling rested is just... not a thing for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have upsetting or disturbing thoughts or images running through your mind while trying to fall asleep, like flashbacks or vivid memories that make it hard to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens when I'm trying to sleep, but honestly, it happens during the day, too. Stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll hear the sound of rocks sliding, or I'll feel my leg stuck under me again, and for a minute I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting at my desk with a headset on. \n\nIt's really embarrassing when it happens at work because I'll just freeze up while I'm on a call. The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. People always threaten to call the Better Business Bureau like it's the FBI or something. Half the time they don't even know what it is. Anyway, she was yelling, and suddenly I just... I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. \n\nMy supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. \n\nI used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know what else to say about the bluff. It happened, and now I'm just trying to deal with it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do these memories or images of the bluff feel very real or as if you're reliving the experience?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they feel completely real. Like I said before, it's like stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. I'll hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I'll literally feel my leg pinned under me, and for a second I'm right back on that trail. It's not like I'm just remembering it, I'm actually there, instead of sitting at my desk with my stupid headset on. \n\nWhich, by the way, I still haven't gotten the old earpiece back. The IT guy—Gary is his name, I finally asked—he actually put a lock on the supply cabinet. A padlock. Like we're going to steal foam earpieces and sell them on the black market or something. He was eating a tuna sandwich when I went to talk to him about it. The whole IT office smelled like fish. I don't understand people who bring fish to an office. It's just common courtesy, you know? Like microwaving broccoli. Just don't do it. My old roommate used to microwave broccoli at like 2 AM. He worked third shift at a warehouse. \n\nBut yeah, when the memories happen, it's... it's like I'm right back there on the dirt, feeling the whole thing happen all over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you have nightmares about what happened on the bluff, or do you often dream about it when you sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's kind of the main reason I came in. I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, thinking about the bluff giving way, or just... hearing the rain. \n\nWhich, by the way, the roof at my apartment building is an absolute joke. Every time it rains, it sounds like the water is coming right through the ceiling. I've told my landlord, Rick, about it like three times. Rick is completely useless. He drives this massive lifted truck that takes up two parking spots, but he can't be bothered to fix a simple leak. He just told me to put a bucket in the hallway. A bucket! Like I'm paying rent to live in a cave. I swear, landlords get away with murder. My neighbor down the hall has a dog that barks at the wall all day, and Rick won't do anything about that either. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to pass out, I have these dreams about the trail. I'll wake up completely sweaty, just drenched, and then a few hours later I have to drag myself out of bed and show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. And then I have to sit there dealing with Brenda and Gary and all the screaming customers on zero sleep. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I definitely won't go near the coast trails now. Not happening. And my hiking buddies keep texting me to hang out or hit a different spot, but I just dodge their texts. Honestly, even just talking about going out there makes my chest tighten up, so I just ignore my phone when they message me. \n\nWhich is actually pretty easy right now because I had to get a new phone last week anyway. I dropped my old one in this massive puddle right outside the call center. The drainage in our parking lot is a total joke. I tried doing that thing where you put the phone in a bag of rice? But it didn't even work, it just got wet rice jammed up inside the charging port. So I had to spend my one day off at the mall dealing with this guy at a cell phone kiosk who kept trying to upsell me on this ridiculous, bulky military-grade case. Like, dude, I sit at a desk getting yelled at about billing errors all day, I don't need a tactical phone case. I just wanted a normal screen protector. The guy wouldn't take no for an answer, though. He was so pushy. \n\nBut yeah, I just don't reply to my friends about the trails anymore. It's too much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The patient denied depressed mood or anhedonia, which makes F32.0 (Mild Depressive Episode) less likely. I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. \n\nMy supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. \n\nI used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know what else to say about the bluff. It happened, and now I'm just trying to deal with it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do these memories or images of the bluff feel very real or as if you're reliving the experience?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they feel completely real. Like I said before, it's like stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. I'll hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I'll literally feel my leg pinned under me, and for a second I'm right back on that trail. It's not like I'm just remembering it, I'm actually there, instead of sitting at my desk with my stupid headset on. \n\nWhich, by the way, I still haven't gotten the old earpiece back. The IT guy—Gary is his name, I finally asked—he actually put a lock on the supply cabinet. A padlock. Like we're going to steal foam earpieces and sell them on the black market or something. He was eating a tuna sandwich when I went to talk to him about it. The whole IT office smelled like fish. I don't understand people who bring fish to an office. It's just common courtesy, you know? Like microwaving broccoli. Just don't do it. My old roommate used to microwave broccoli at like 2 AM. He worked third shift at a warehouse. \n\nBut yeah, when the memories happen, it's... it's like I'm right back there on the dirt, feeling the whole thing happen all over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you have nightmares about what happened on the bluff, or do you often dream about it when you sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's kind of the main reason I came in. Like I'm paying rent to live in a cave. I swear, landlords get away with murder. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it really has. My work has definitely been slipping lately. I actually got written up the other day because I missed putting some important notes in the CRM system after a call escalated. 

Which, honestly, our CRM software is an absolute joke. It looks like it was programmed in 1995. It takes five minutes just to load a customer's account, and by then, they're already screaming at you. This guy on the phone was losing his mind over a twelve-dollar late fee. Twelve dollars! People act like you personally reached into their bank account and robbed them. And my supervisor didn't even back me up when things got heated. So now I'm pretty sure I completely blew my chance at moving toward a team lead position. I really needed that bump in pay, too, because groceries are getting ridiculous. Have you bought eggs recently? It's like they're made of gold now. I went to the store on Tuesday and just walked right past the dairy aisle. I don't know how anyone affords to eat anything but ramen these days. 

But anyway, yeah, it's affecting my personal stuff too. I've totally fallen behind on my GED studying. I'm just so exhausted all the time that I can't even look at the prep book when I get home. It's this massive, heavy textbook. I tried taking it to the coffee shop on the corner to study and get out of my leaky apartment, but they play that weird, plinky acoustic music so loud you can't even hear yourself think. Plus the barista always spells my name wrong on the cup, even when I'm the only person in the store. 

So between being completely drained and just... avoiding the people I used to hike with, I'm pretty much just going to work, getting yelled at, and going home to do nothing. I just don't have the energy for any of it.

That's actually why I came in, because I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. I'll just lie there for hours, hearing the rain against the window, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way... and then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from these intense dreams. \n\nI used to love the rain, actually. When I was a kid, my grandma had this tin roof on her back porch and we'd sit out there just listening to it. She used to make this incredible sweet tea, like, diabetes in a glass, but it was so good. She kept it in this giant glass pitcher with yellow lemons painted on the side. I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do manage to sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's pretty much what I just said. Like, I'll get maybe three, four hours if I'm lucky, and I still feel like I just ran a marathon when my alarm goes off. My alarm clock is actually this old radio I got at a garage sale. It's got this really harsh buzzing sound that just jars you awake. I've been meaning to use my phone instead, but I always forget to charge it. \n\nI actually need to get a new charger cable because Barnaby chewed through my last one. He's got this weird obsession with cords. I had to wrap all the lamp cords in that plastic tubing stuff, which was a huge pain to put on. I bought it at the hardware store down the street, the one next to that bakery that makes those amazing cheese danishes. Man, I haven't had one of those in ages. The lady who runs it is always so nice, she always gives me an extra one if I come in right before they close. \n\nBut yeah, the sleep thing. It's just... it's never enough. I always feel completely wiped out, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I feel like we're going in circles here, doc, but yes. That's literally why I made the appointment. I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. And then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from those dreams. So yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never slept at all. The new ones have this foam earpiece that makes my ear itch like crazy. I tried swapping it out with the old one, but the IT guy got super mad about it. He's this really intense dude who wears suspenders every day. Who wears suspenders to fix computers? He also collects those little bobblehead figures. His whole desk is covered in them. Every time you walk by, they all start nodding at you. It's honestly a little unnerving. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, waking up feeling rested is just... not a thing for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have upsetting or disturbing thoughts or images running through your mind while trying to fall asleep, like flashbacks or vivid memories that make it hard to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens when I'm trying to sleep, but honestly, it happens during the day, too. Stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll hear the sound of rocks sliding, or I'll feel my leg stuck under me again, and for a minute I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting at my desk with a headset on. \n\nIt's really embarrassing when it happens at work because I'll just freeze up while I'm on a call. The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know what else to say about the bluff. Like I'm paying rent to live in a cave. I swear, landlords get away with murder. It's too much.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep trouble or these upsetting thoughts about the bluff made it hard for you to do your job, be around other people, or take care of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I went to the store on Tuesday and just walked right past the dairy aisle. Yeah, definitely. Stuff just pops into my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll be sitting there at my desk, trying to listen to someone complain about their bill, and suddenly I just hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I feel exactly how my leg felt when it was stuck under me. Which, by the way, those headsets they give us at the call center are the absolute worst. The foam on the earpiece is always peeling off, and they pinch your head so bad. I've asked IT for a new one like four times, but they act like I'm asking for a solid gold crown. The IT guy, Kevin, always tells me to submit a ticket, but the ticketing portal is always down. It's like, how am I supposed to submit a ticket about the broken portal when the portal is broken? Kevin just sits in his little office all day eating those smelly cheese puffs. You can smell them all the way down the hall. I swear, half my job is just trying not to breathe through my nose when I walk past his door. 

But yeah, when those memories hit me like that, it totally derails me. I'll completely lose track of what the customer is saying, and then they get even madder. It's just really hard to focus on anything else when it happens.

I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. Like, I'll get maybe three, four hours if I'm lucky, and I still feel like I just ran a marathon when my alarm goes off. My alarm clock is actually this old radio I got at a garage sale. It's got this really harsh buzzing sound that just jars you awake. I've been meaning to use my phone instead, but I always forget to charge it. \n\nI actually need to get a new charger cable because Barnaby chewed through my last one. He's got this weird obsession with cords. I had to wrap all the lamp cords in that plastic tubing stuff, which was a huge pain to put on. I bought it at the hardware store down the street, the one next to that bakery that makes those amazing cheese danishes. Man, I haven't had one of those in ages. The lady who runs it is always so nice, she always gives me an extra one if I come in right before they close. \n\nBut yeah, the sleep thing. It's just... it's never enough. I always feel completely wiped out, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do sleep for a few hours, do you still wake up feeling like you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I feel like we're going in circles here, doc, but yes. That's literally why I made the appointment. I barely sleep right anymore. Even if I get a few hours, it's not restful at all. I'll just lie there for hours, hearing the rain against the window, or I start thinking about the bluff giving way... and then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from these intense dreams. \n\nI used to love the rain, actually. When I was a kid, my grandma had this tin roof on her back porch and we'd sit out there just listening to it. She used to make this incredible sweet tea, like, diabetes in a glass, but it was so good. She kept it in this giant glass pitcher with yellow lemons painted on the side. I tried to find one like it at a thrift store once, but all they had were those creepy porcelain dolls that look like they're staring at you. I don't know who buys those things. They look like they're gonna come alive in the middle of the night and murder you. \n\nAnyway, yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that even after sleeping, your mind feels foggy or your body feels heavy and tired throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, foggy is a good word for it. Like I said, I came in because I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, hearing the rain or just... thinking about the bluff giving way, you know? And then when I finally do pass out, I wake up all sweaty from those dreams. So yeah, I show up to the call center feeling like I never slept at all. My body just feels like lead. \n\nHonestly, sitting in that chair all day doesn't help. The chairs at work are terrible. They're those cheap mesh ones that squeak every time you shift your weight. The guy in the cubicle next to me, Gary, he leans back so far I swear he's gonna snap the thing in half one day. Gary's always bringing in these weird smelling lunches, too. Like microwaved fish or something. I don't know how anyone can eat that at 11 in the morning. I usually just grab a bag of chips from the vending machine, but half the time the coils get stuck and you have to shake the whole machine. \n\nBut yeah, my mind is definitely foggy. It's like trying to think through mud all day.\n\nDoctor: Even when you do manage to sleep for a few hours, do you wake up feeling as though you haven’t really rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I feel like we're just saying the same thing over and over, doc. But yes. Like I said, I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there for hours, listening to the rain or my brain just goes right back to the bluff giving way. And when I finally do fall asleep, I just wake up drenched in sweat from these awful dreams. So by the time I have to drag myself into the call center, it feels like I didn't sleep a single minute. \n\nSpeaking of the call center, they just changed our headsets again. The new ones have this foam earpiece that makes my ear itch like crazy. I tried swapping it out with the old one, but the IT guy got super mad about it. He's this really intense dude who wears suspenders every day. Who wears suspenders to fix computers? He also collects those little bobblehead figures. His whole desk is covered in them. Every time you walk by, they all start nodding at you. It's honestly a little unnerving. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, waking up feeling rested is just... not a thing for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have upsetting or disturbing thoughts or images running through your mind while trying to fall asleep, like flashbacks or vivid memories that make it hard to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens when I'm trying to sleep, but honestly, it happens during the day, too. Stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll hear the sound of rocks sliding, or I'll feel my leg stuck under me again, and for a minute I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting at my desk with a headset on. \n\nIt's really embarrassing when it happens at work because I'll just freeze up while I'm on a call. The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. People always threaten to call the Better Business Bureau like it's the FBI or something. Half the time they don't even know what it is. Anyway, she was yelling, and suddenly I just... I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. \n\nMy supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. \n\nI used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know what else to say about the bluff. It happened, and now I'm just trying to deal with it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do these memories or images of the bluff feel very real or as if you're reliving the experience?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they feel completely real. Like I said before, it's like stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. I'll hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I'll literally feel my leg pinned under me, and for a second I'm right back on that trail. It's not like I'm just remembering it, I'm actually there, instead of sitting at my desk with my stupid headset on. \n\nWhich, by the way, I still haven't gotten the old earpiece back. The IT guy—Gary is his name, I finally asked—he actually put a lock on the supply cabinet. A padlock. Like we're going to steal foam earpieces and sell them on the black market or something. He was eating a tuna sandwich when I went to talk to him about it. The whole IT office smelled like fish. I don't understand people who bring fish to an office. It's just common courtesy, you know? Like microwaving broccoli. Just don't do it. My old roommate used to microwave broccoli at like 2 AM. He worked third shift at a warehouse. \n\nBut yeah, when the memories happen, it's... it's like I'm right back there on the dirt, feeling the whole thing happen all over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you have nightmares about what happened on the bluff, or do you often dream about it when you sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's kind of the main reason I came in. I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, thinking about the bluff giving way, or just... hearing the rain. \n\nWhich, by the way, the roof at my apartment building is an absolute joke. Every time it rains, it sounds like the water is coming right through the ceiling. I've told my landlord, Rick, about it like three times. Rick is completely useless. He drives this massive lifted truck that takes up two parking spots, but he can't be bothered to fix a simple leak. He just told me to put a bucket in the hallway. A bucket! Like I'm paying rent to live in a cave. I swear, landlords get away with murder. My neighbor down the hall has a dog that barks at the wall all day, and Rick won't do anything about that either. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to pass out, I have these dreams about the trail. I'll wake up completely sweaty, just drenched, and then a few hours later I have to drag myself out of bed and show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. And then I have to sit there dealing with Brenda and Gary and all the screaming customers on zero sleep. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I definitely won't go near the coast trails now. Not happening. And my hiking buddies keep texting me to hang out or hit a different spot, but I just dodge their texts. Honestly, even just talking about going out there makes my chest tighten up, so I just ignore my phone when they message me. \n\nWhich is actually pretty easy right now because I had to get a new phone last week anyway. I dropped my old one in this massive puddle right outside the call center. The drainage in our parking lot is a total joke. I tried doing that thing where you put the phone in a bag of rice? But it didn't even work, it just got wet rice jammed up inside the charging port. So I had to spend my one day off at the mall dealing with this guy at a cell phone kiosk who kept trying to upsell me on this ridiculous, bulky military-grade case. Like, dude, I sit at a desk getting yelled at about billing errors all day, I don't need a tactical phone case. I just wanted a normal screen protector. The guy wouldn't take no for an answer, though. He was so pushy. \n\nBut yeah, I just don't reply to my friends about the trails anymore. It's too much.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep trouble or these upsetting thoughts about the bluff made it hard for you to do your job, be around other people, or take care of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. My work has definitely been slipping lately. I actually got written up the other day because I missed putting some important notes in the CRM system after a call escalated. \n\nWhich, honestly, our CRM software is an absolute joke. It looks like it was programmed in 1995. It takes five minutes just to load a customer's account, and by then, they're already screaming at you. This guy on the phone was losing his mind over a twelve-dollar late fee. Twelve dollars! People act like you personally reached into their bank account and robbed them. And my supervisor didn't even back me up when things got heated. So now I'm pretty sure I completely blew my chance at moving toward a team lead position. I really needed that bump in pay, too, because groceries are getting ridiculous. Have you bought eggs recently? It's like they're made of gold now. I went to the store on Tuesday and just walked right past the dairy aisle. I don't know how anyone affords to eat anything but ramen these days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, it's affecting my personal stuff too. I've totally fallen behind on my GED studying. I'm just so exhausted all the time that I can't even look at the prep book when I get home. It's this massive, heavy textbook. I tried taking it to the coffee shop on the corner to study and get out of my leaky apartment, but they play that weird, plinky acoustic music so loud you can't even hear yourself think. Plus the barista always spells my name wrong on the cup, even when I'm the only person in the store. \n\nSo between being completely drained and just... avoiding the people I used to hike with, I'm pretty much just going to work, getting yelled at, and going home to do nothing. I just don't have the energy for any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts or memories of the bluff incident come up so often or feel so intense that they interfere with your ability to focus or function normally during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Stuff just pops into my head out of nowhere. The IT guy, Kevin, always tells me to submit a ticket, but the ticketing portal is always down. It's like, how am I supposed to submit a ticket about the broken portal when the portal is broken? Kevin just sits in his little office all day eating those smelly cheese puffs. You can smell them all the way down the hall. I swear, half my job is just trying not to breathe through my nose when I walk past his door. \n\nBut yeah, when those memories hit me like that, it totally derails me. I'll completely lose track of what the customer is saying, and then they get even madder. It's just really hard to focus on anything else when it happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah, for sure. My patience is just... it's way shorter than it used to be. Like at work, I've been snapping at customers over really small stuff, which I never really used to do. I actually raised my voice at this one guy enough that my supervisor had to pull me aside and give me a talking to.

And when I finally do fall asleep, I just wake up drenched in sweat from these awful dreams. So by the time I have to drag myself into the call center, it feels like I didn't sleep a single minute. \n\nSpeaking of the call center, they just changed our headsets again. The new ones have this foam earpiece that makes my ear itch like crazy. I tried swapping it out with the old one, but the IT guy got super mad about it. He's this really intense dude who wears suspenders every day. Who wears suspenders to fix computers? He also collects those little bobblehead figures. His whole desk is covered in them. Every time you walk by, they all start nodding at you. It's honestly a little unnerving. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, waking up feeling rested is just... not a thing for me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have upsetting or disturbing thoughts or images running through your mind while trying to fall asleep, like flashbacks or vivid memories that make it hard to rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens when I'm trying to sleep, but honestly, it happens during the day, too. Stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll hear the sound of rocks sliding, or I'll feel my leg stuck under me again, and for a minute I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting at my desk with a headset on. \n\nIt's really embarrassing when it happens at work because I'll just freeze up while I'm on a call. The other day I was talking to this lady who was furious about her internet bill. She was screaming about how we were scamming her and how she was going to call the Better Business Bureau. People always threaten to call the Better Business Bureau like it's the FBI or something. Half the time they don't even know what it is. Anyway, she was yelling, and suddenly I just... I wasn't there anymore. I was back on the bluff. \n\nMy supervisor, Brenda, had to tap me on the shoulder to snap me out of it. Brenda's always hovering, though. She wears this really strong perfume that smells like baby powder and roses. It gives me a headache if she stands behind my chair too long. She also clicks her pen constantly. Click, click, click. It drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, the memories and images... they just hit me, whether I'm trying to sleep or just trying to get through a shift.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really want to get into all the details of it, if that's okay. It was just... I was hiking, the ground gave way, and I got trapped. That's pretty much it. \n\nI used to love hiking, though. Before I moved here, I lived near this state park that had the best trails. There was this one trail that led up to a waterfall, and in the summer, you could swim in the pool at the bottom. The water was always freezing, even in July. My buddy Mike and I used to go up there every weekend. Mike actually moved to Florida a couple of years ago. He works at a car dealership now, selling used Hondas or something. He keeps trying to get me to move down there, says the weather is way better. But I hate the humidity. Plus, Florida has all those giant bugs. I saw this video online of a palmetto bug that was like, the size of a rat. No thank you. \n\nAnyway, I don't really know what else to say about the bluff. It happened, and now I'm just trying to deal with it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do these memories or images of the bluff feel very real or as if you're reliving the experience?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they feel completely real. Like I said before, it's like stuff just pops in my head out of nowhere. I'll hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I'll literally feel my leg pinned under me, and for a second I'm right back on that trail. It's not like I'm just remembering it, I'm actually there, instead of sitting at my desk with my stupid headset on. \n\nWhich, by the way, I still haven't gotten the old earpiece back. The IT guy—Gary is his name, I finally asked—he actually put a lock on the supply cabinet. A padlock. Like we're going to steal foam earpieces and sell them on the black market or something. He was eating a tuna sandwich when I went to talk to him about it. The whole IT office smelled like fish. I don't understand people who bring fish to an office. It's just common courtesy, you know? Like microwaving broccoli. Just don't do it. My old roommate used to microwave broccoli at like 2 AM. He worked third shift at a warehouse. \n\nBut yeah, when the memories happen, it's... it's like I'm right back there on the dirt, feeling the whole thing happen all over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you have nightmares about what happened on the bluff, or do you often dream about it when you sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's kind of the main reason I came in. I barely sleep right anymore. I'll just lie there in the dark, thinking about the bluff giving way, or just... hearing the rain. \n\nWhich, by the way, the roof at my apartment building is an absolute joke. Every time it rains, it sounds like the water is coming right through the ceiling. I've told my landlord, Rick, about it like three times. Rick is completely useless. He drives this massive lifted truck that takes up two parking spots, but he can't be bothered to fix a simple leak. He just told me to put a bucket in the hallway. A bucket! Like I'm paying rent to live in a cave. I swear, landlords get away with murder. My neighbor down the hall has a dog that barks at the wall all day, and Rick won't do anything about that either. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to pass out, I have these dreams about the trail. I'll wake up completely sweaty, just drenched, and then a few hours later I have to drag myself out of bed and show up to the call center feeling like I never even slept. And then I have to sit there dealing with Brenda and Gary and all the screaming customers on zero sleep. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of what happened on the bluff?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I definitely won't go near the coast trails now. Not happening. And my hiking buddies keep texting me to hang out or hit a different spot, but I just dodge their texts. Honestly, even just talking about going out there makes my chest tighten up, so I just ignore my phone when they message me. \n\nWhich is actually pretty easy right now because I had to get a new phone last week anyway. I dropped my old one in this massive puddle right outside the call center. The drainage in our parking lot is a total joke. I tried doing that thing where you put the phone in a bag of rice? But it didn't even work, it just got wet rice jammed up inside the charging port. So I had to spend my one day off at the mall dealing with this guy at a cell phone kiosk who kept trying to upsell me on this ridiculous, bulky military-grade case. Like, dude, I sit at a desk getting yelled at about billing errors all day, I don't need a tactical phone case. I just wanted a normal screen protector. The guy wouldn't take no for an answer, though. He was so pushy. \n\nBut yeah, I just don't reply to my friends about the trails anymore. It's too much.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep trouble or these upsetting thoughts about the bluff made it hard for you to do your job, be around other people, or take care of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. My work has definitely been slipping lately. I actually got written up the other day because I missed putting some important notes in the CRM system after a call escalated. \n\nWhich, honestly, our CRM software is an absolute joke. It looks like it was programmed in 1995. It takes five minutes just to load a customer's account, and by then, they're already screaming at you. This guy on the phone was losing his mind over a twelve-dollar late fee. Twelve dollars! People act like you personally reached into their bank account and robbed them. And my supervisor didn't even back me up when things got heated. So now I'm pretty sure I completely blew my chance at moving toward a team lead position. I really needed that bump in pay, too, because groceries are getting ridiculous. Have you bought eggs recently? It's like they're made of gold now. I went to the store on Tuesday and just walked right past the dairy aisle. I don't know how anyone affords to eat anything but ramen these days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, it's affecting my personal stuff too. I've totally fallen behind on my GED studying. I'm just so exhausted all the time that I can't even look at the prep book when I get home. It's this massive, heavy textbook. I tried taking it to the coffee shop on the corner to study and get out of my leaky apartment, but they play that weird, plinky acoustic music so loud you can't even hear yourself think. Plus the barista always spells my name wrong on the cup, even when I'm the only person in the store. \n\nSo between being completely drained and just... avoiding the people I used to hike with, I'm pretty much just going to work, getting yelled at, and going home to do nothing. I just don't have the energy for any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that thoughts or memories of the bluff incident come up so often or feel so intense that they interfere with your ability to focus or function normally during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Stuff just pops into my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll be sitting there at my desk, trying to listen to someone complain about their bill, and suddenly I just hear the sound of the rocks sliding, or I feel exactly how my leg felt when it was stuck under me. For a minute, it's like I'm right back on that trail instead of sitting there with my headset on. \n\nWhich, by the way, those headsets they give us at the call center are the absolute worst. The foam on the earpiece is always peeling off, and they pinch your head so bad. I've asked IT for a new one like four times, but they act like I'm asking for a solid gold crown. The IT guy, Kevin, always tells me to submit a ticket, but the ticketing portal is always down. It's like, how am I supposed to submit a ticket about the broken portal when the portal is broken? Kevin just sits in his little office all day eating those smelly cheese puffs. You can smell them all the way down the hall. I swear, half my job is just trying not to breathe through my nose when I walk past his door. \n\nBut yeah, when those memories hit me like that, it totally derails me. I'll completely lose track of what the customer is saying, and then they get even madder. It's just really hard to focus on anything else when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling unusually on edge, irritable, or easily startled since the incident on the bluff?\n\nPatient: Yeah, for sure. My patience is just... it's way shorter than it used to be. Like at work, I've been snapping at customers over really small stuff, which I never really used to do. I actually raised my voice at this one guy enough that my supervisor had to pull me aside and give me a talking to. \n\nWhich is so rich coming from him, honestly. Dave—that's my supervisor—he literally yells at the vending machine in the breakroom if his snacks get stuck. I saw him shoulder-check the machine last week over a bag of jalapeño kettle chips. Which, by the way, are disgusting. They just taste like spicy dust. But he shook it so hard I thought the glass was gonna shatter. And the guy who restocks that machine is completely useless anyway. He always parks his van right across two handicap spaces out front, and he leaves all his empty cardboard boxes piled up by the dumpster instead of breaking them down. The property manager sent out this massive, passive-aggressive email about the boxes last month, but they CC'd the whole company instead of BCC'ing, so then everyone started hitting 'reply all' with these stupid jokes. My inbox was basically unusable for like three hours just pinging non-stop. \n\nBut, uh, yeah... I guess I've just been really on edge and irritable lately."
